# Oxygen full code review, November 2026

A review of `main` at `df5460c3` (still the head of `main` on 2026-11-24), plus the unmerged PR #254 (course elevation and leg routes, branch `claude/project-thread-avzbks`) wherever it touches an area; area 09 reviewed PR #254 at its head `871c74de`. Review only: no code was changed, no PR was opened, and the dev database on :5432 was not touched.

**How it was made.** The codebase was split into 11 areas along its real seams. Each area had one to seven Fable reviewers who read their slice in full, and every finding then went to a separate Fable verifier that saw only the claim and its location, never the reviewer's reasoning, and was asked to disprove it. Verifiers read the code and, where it was cheap, ran a scratch script, a throwaway-Postgres reproduction (on :5433, through `scripts/with-verify-lock.sh`) or a scratch vitest. Findings that did not survive were dropped (listed under [Left out on purpose](#left-out-on-purpose)). This file merges the 11 area reports: duplicates found by several areas are folded into one entry that keeps every ID, and the result is ordered by severity with the focus Marcus asked for: data loss or corruption, one competition's data reaching another, and anything that stops the system mid-event.

**How to use it as a work queue.**
- Every entry keeps its original area ID (API-, MC-, DB-, RES-, SI-, SYNC-, EVT-, MAP-, CRS-, WEB-, BLD-). A merged entry is filed under one ID and lists the others under "Also reported as"; fix them as one item and close all the IDs.
- Critical and high findings are written out below with location, what is wrong, a scenario, the fix and how it was checked. Medium and low findings are compact table rows with the location, the problem (with the scenario for medium), a fix hint and the size.
- Every ID links to its full entry in `docs/reviews/areas/NN-*.md` (same branch), which has the complete location list, scenario, fix, test to add and the verifier's evidence. Area 09's untruncated verifier evidence is in the project folder `reviews/09-courses-printing-raw.json`.
- Text inside an entry is taken from the area report; a "…" marks where it was shortened for this file.
- Line numbers are for `df5460c3` (PR #254 lines are for `871c74de`).
- Some area entries bundle two defects. Where only part of an entry is a duplicate, it is filed under its main defect and the other part is named under "Related" (for example DB-21 and MC-42 also contain RES-17, DB-18's second half is SYNC-08, DB-44 overlaps SI-38, API-37f and MC-37). Close an ID only when every part of its full entry is fixed.
- The deduplication was checked by a separate Fable agent that read the area entries for every merged group and looked for missed duplicates; its corrections are applied.
- Before fixing anything listed under [Decisions that need Marcus](#decisions-that-need-marcus), get the answer and record it in the spec first (CLAUDE.md rule).

"Confirmed" means reproduced or proven from the code end to end. "Confirmed, part plausible" means the mechanism is proven but one part of the impact depends on something that could not be observed (real Eventor or LiveResults behaviour, a hardware detail, a timing). "Plausible" means not disproven and not proven. Where duplicates disagreed, the merged entry takes the highest severity a verifier gave. One severity was raised in the merge: **EVT-01** (Eventor re-sync cancelling runners), which areas 01, 02, 03 and 07 rated high and area 01's verifier called "arguably critical"; one unconfirmed button press on race day can turn every pre-entered runner, finished ones included, into Cancelled and the next sync loses their status, which is data loss in normal use.

The overlap was heaviest where the areas meet: the journal sink `events.push` (reported by areas 01, 02, 03, 05, 06 and 11), journal replay of `result.applied` (five areas), Eventor sync (four areas), the backup (four areas) and the ROC radio feed (four areas). The overlaps the coordinator flagged are merged as follows: SYNC-06 = RES-07 (filed under RES-07 with API-09, DB-03 and SI-08); WEB-01 = SYNC-19 (filed under MC-04, the per-competition query cache); EVT-02 and RES-02 stay separate because they are different defects in the same function (EVT-02 reverts desk edits with no results involved; RES-02 stores Eventor's clock times in ZeroTime-relative columns), so fix them in one change.

**What the review says overall.** The per-router event scoping, the procedure builders, the map-document storage and the course and map write transactions are sound; nearly every serious finding sits on a path that bypasses them. Four root causes produce most of the critical and high findings:

1. **The journal sink trusts its input.** `events.push` applies whatever it is sent: to the event named in the request header rather than the entry's own competition, with no capability check, addressing reference rows by bare UUID, and replaying `result.applied` with a status mapping that knows only OK, MP and DNF (MC-01, API-01, API-03, RES-07, SYNC-20).
2. **The web client binds a write to a competition when it is sent, not when it is made.** The header comes from `window.location` at send time and the query cache is not keyed by competition, so queued writes, cached rows and offline readouts can cross competitions (MC-01, MC-04, MC-17, MC-05, WEB-02, SYNC-25).
3. **There is no single server step that applies a readout.** Storing a card, evaluating it and writing the result happen in different places for the admin shell, the kiosk, the finish station, the station-backup push and the journal, and they disagree: a re-read overwrites a DQ, a manual finish is erased, an offline readout queues no result (RES-01, SYNC-03, SI-10, SI-11, RES-12).
4. **Venue and cloud sync has gaps at its edges.** The shipper's HLC cursor skips late entries and the checkin barrier cannot see them, sequence counters do not travel back, checkout deletes unshipped data, and a box without a lease still writes (MC-02, DB-01, API-07, SYNC-08).

## Fix these five first

1. **Offline and kiosk readouts are lost: SYNC-03 (with SI-02, SI-03, MC-06), fixed together with MC-04 and MC-19.** When the server is unreachable a standalone kiosk stores and queues nothing while still showing the runner a screen, and in every tab the offline `result.applied` emit is dead code, so after the network returns the runner has a card but no result. Fixing the emit without first keying the query cache by competition (MC-04) would turn the cached-data mix-up into server-side corruption, so do both. Size M + M.
2. **A re-read overwrites the stored result: RES-01 (with SI-01), together with SI-10 and SI-11.** A cleared hire card turns OK into DNF, a re-read after a jury DQ brings the runner back as OK, and a later read erases a manual finish. Build the one server-side "store, evaluate, apply under guards" helper the area 05 review proposes and use it from `storeReadout`, the `card.read` ingest and `pushReadoutBackup`. Size M.
3. **Harden the journal sink in one change: MC-01 (with API-02, BLD-04), API-01 (with MC-03, SI-07, SYNC-04), API-03 and RES-07.** Reject entries whose `competitionId` is not the request's event and make idempotency event-aware; have the client send the header of the entries, not of the URL; require a capability per entry type (the peer secret bypasses it); scope reference upserts to the event and whitelist their fields; map every status in `result.applied` replay. Size M (server S, client M). Needs the capability-map decision D1 below.
4. **Venue/cloud lease integrity: MC-02 (with DB-02, SYNC-01), DB-01 (with SYNC-02) and API-07 (with MC-09, SYNC-07, DB-18).** Ship by local arrival order instead of HLC so a station that drains late is not skipped, raise the per-event `seq` counter on explicit-seq inserts so creates do not fail after checkin, and refuse checkout over unshipped journal entries. Each of these loses race data on a venue + cloud event without any warning. Size M + S–M + M.
5. **Eventor sync on race day: EVT-01 (with API-04, API-10, MC-12, DB-08), EVT-02 and RES-02, plus EVT-03.** One press of "Sync from Eventor" to pull late entries can cancel every runner (an empty or odd 200 response), cancels runners who already finished, reverts desk edits (a rental card back to NULL, a class move undone), and after results were uploaded shifts every time by ZeroTime. Guard the empty response, never cancel or overwrite a runner with a result or a local edit, and convert times with `toRelative`. Size S + M + M. Needs decision D2 below.

These fixes change behaviour that a spec or doc describes on purpose, or the spec is silent, so per CLAUDE.md the answer goes into the spec (and a decision-log row where a research decision is changed) before any code.

| # | Question | Findings | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Which capability does each journal entry type need in `events.push`, and does the peer (sync-secret) push bypass the map? Should registration desks that queue `runner.*` need `race.operate` or `event.manage`? | API-01, DB-04, SYNC-23 | `docs/system/authentication.md:231` documents the open sink as intended. Area 02 asks for a two-node proxy-mode run first, to see whether venue-to-cloud push works behind IAP today. |
| D2 | What may an Eventor re-sync overwrite after a local edit, and may it overwrite local results at all? | EVT-02, DB-08, RES-02, EVT-01 | Suggested by the reviewers: never null a locally set card, never revert class or name, never touch a runner with a readout or a non-Unknown status. |
| D3 | Course import semantics: should re-import keep a matched control's status, extra codes and name; may "replace all" become one transaction (a recorded decision says it is not); what happens to duplicate ids, a class mapped to several courses, and a file with several RaceCourseData? | CRS-001, CRS-022, CRS-060, CRS-008, CRS-034 | docs/course-layer.md §6.4 is silent or says otherwise. |

## Findings

### Critical (9)

#### SYNC-03 A standalone kiosk drops card readouts when the node is unreachable, and the offline `result.applied` emit is dead code

*Area 06 (Offline sync and publishing) · stability (data loss) · **critical** · size M · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [SI-02](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-02-an-offline-or-failed-fetch-readout-never-queues-resultapplied-stale-closure), [SI-03](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-03-a-standalone-kiosk-queues-nothing-when-the-server-is-unreachable), [MC-06](areas/02-multi-competition.md#mc-06-offline-readouts-never-queue-resultapplied-stale-closure-after-sync-the-runner-has-a-card-but-no-result)
- **Related:** MC-04, SI-15, SI-29, SYNC-24, BLD-23
- **Location:** `packages/web/src/context/DeviceManager.tsx:252-253,268-270`: `competitionNameIdRef` starts `null`; `setCompetitionNameId` is its only writer. `packages/web/src/pages/CompetitionShell.tsx:233,258`: the only callers. `packages/web/src/App.tsx:95-96`: `/:nameId/kiosk` and `/:nameId/start-screen` are routed outside `CompetitionShell`. …
- **What is wrong:** On the kiosk route the ref is `null`, so when `storeReadout` fails or the browser is offline, `card.read` is neither stored nor queued. Only a `console.warn` remains. The kiosk route also mounts no queue drain and no pending or failed badge. The offline local-readout branch reads a stale closure value that is always `null`, so it never emits `result.applied`, in any tab including the admin shell. Re-reading the card does not repair it: the earlier read is still in `recentCardsRef`, so it is treated as a duplicate (`:317-320`) and skipped at `:358` and `:418`, although it was never stored.
- **Scenario:** a standalone kiosk tablet at the finish, Wi-Fi blips for 2 minutes, 15 runners read out. None of the 15 `card.read`s exist anywhere. Re-reading the same cards at the same tab is skipped as duplicate; recovery needs a reload, another device, or 20 newer cards. On a kiosk-only device the screen typically shows "register" or "pre-start" rather than a result (`computeCardReadout` needs a cached `runner.list`), but never says the readout was not saved.
- **Fix (M):** Derive the competition for queued events from the URL (export `activeEventNameId()` from `lib/trpc.ts`), or have `KioskPage` and `StartScreenPage` call `setCompetitionNameId(nameId)`. Replace `competitionNameId` at `:572-573` with the same source. Do this together with SYNC-19, or the A-cache corruption becomes real. Do not mark a card as a duplicate unless it was stored or queued. Mount `useEventQueue(nameId)` headless in `KioskPage` with a small pending or failed badge; if no competition can be determined, show an error instead of skipping. Unit test `addRecentCard` with `storeReadout` rejecting in a kiosk-only tree; E2E `e2e/kiosk-readout.spec.ts` with `context.setOffline(true)`.
- **Status:** confirmed from code reading (grep shows `setCompetitionNameId` called only in the shell). The verifier narrowed "the kiosk shows a locally computed result" (that needs `runner.list` cached in the same browser profile within 24 h) and added the …
- **Full entry:** [SYNC-03](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-03-a-standalone-kiosk-drops-card-readouts-when-the-node-is-unreachable-and-the-offline-resultapplied-emit-is-dead-code)

#### RES-01 Re-reading a card overwrites an existing result

*Area 04 (ZeroTime, results and draw) · functional, stability · **critical** · size M · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [SI-01](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-01-a-re-read-overwrites-the-stored-result-a-cleared-card-wipes-an-ok-result-and-a-re-read-undoes-a-dq)
- **Related:** SI-10, SI-11
- **Location:** `packages/web/src/context/DeviceManager.tsx:449-466`: `hasDbResult` forces `action = "readout"`. `DeviceManager.tsx:503-510`: auto-apply, no dialog. `DeviceManager.tsx:519-529`: offline fallback queues the same values. `packages/api/src/routers/cardReadout.ts:805-856`: `applyResult` selects `runner.status` but never uses it. …
- **What is wrong:** whenever the runner already has a result, any read of the card is applied as a readout, with no confirmation. The status, start and finish are recomputed from the card and written over whatever is stored. `computeStatus` uses the current status only when the run has a finish, no missing controls and a running time of 0 or less. The duplicate check (`DeviceManager.tsx:316-320`) only skips `storeReadout`; `applyResult` still runs. The freshness check (`:186-215`) only affects what is displayed. Scenarios (all confirmed from code): A jury DQs a runner, who then reads the card again for a second receipt. The status goes back to OK and the runner is ranked. The same happens to any manually set status. An organiser corrects MP to OK by hand. A re-read sets it back to MP. …
- **Fix (M):** move the decision server-side into one `applyReadout` mutation. Never overwrite DQ, or a status or time marked as manual. Add a `statusManual` flag set by `runner.update` and the status buttons. Refuse to replace a result when the new read has no finish, an empty punch list, or `punchesFresh === false`. Return `needsConfirmation` and have the UI ask. Do not overwrite `cards.punches_raw` with an empty read once the runner has a result. Add integration tests for each scenario above.
- **Status:** confirmed (proven from code). The reviewer reproduced `computeStatus` returning OK over DQ, and DNF for an empty card. The verifier walked each scenario and found no guard; the stale-card scenario is narrower than first claimed, as described above.
- **Full entry:** [RES-01](areas/04-zerotime-results.md#res-01-re-reading-a-card-overwrites-an-existing-result)

#### MC-02 The journal shipper's HLC watermark skips entries that are ingested late; checkin then reports 0 pending

*Area 02 (Multi-competition isolation) · stability / data loss · **critical** · size M · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [DB-02](areas/03-prisma-migrations.md#db-02-journal-shipper-skips-entries-that-arrive-below-its-hlc-watermark), [SYNC-01](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-01-journal-shipping-skips-entries-that-land-below-the-hlc-id-watermark-and-the-checkin-barrier-cannot-see-them)
- **Related:** API-07, SYNC-08
- **Location:** `packages/api/src/sync/shipper.ts:194-220` (push) and `:231-254` (pull, same flaw). `packages/api/src/routers/events.ts:185-196`: the `listJournalEntriesSince` predicate `hlc > cursor`. `events.ts:110-131`: an ingested entry is stored under the station's creation-time HLC (stamped at `packages/web/src/lib/offline/events.ts:59`). …
- **What is wrong:** Push and pull select `hlc > watermark` and advance the watermark to the HLC of the last acknowledged entry. A station entry keeps its own, older HLC when the box ingests it. `foldServerHlc` only moves the node clock forward and does not restamp. Any entry that reaches the box after a newer entry has already shipped is never selected again. The shipper runs every 5 s (`nodeIdentity.ts:50-53`), so no long outage is needed. `shippingStatus` uses the same predicate and reports 0 pending. `docs/system/offline-architecture.md:317-319` claims "a watermark never skips past an unapplied entry". That is false.
- **Scenario:** A start station's Wi-Fi drops for 30 s and it records 3 entries stamped 09:05:10. Meanwhile the box ships its own 09:05:20 entry, so the watermark is 09:05:20. The station drains at 09:05:40. Its entries land with HLC 09:05:10 and are never pushed. Checkin passes. The cloud lacks them. The next checkout runs `tx.event.deleteMany` on the venue copy (`sync/lease.ts:319`), and the entries are gone for good.
- **Fix (M):** Ship by local arrival order, not by HLC. Add a per-node monotonic `ingest_seq BIGSERIAL` column on `journal` with an index on `(event_id, ingest_seq)`. Make the shipper cursor, `shippingStatus` and the snapshot `journalCursor` all use it. Keep `(hlc, id)` only as the apply order. Correct the doc. Add a two-node integration test that ingests an entry with an HLC below the watermark and asserts it ships.
- **Status:** confirmed from code.
- **Full entry:** [MC-02](areas/02-multi-competition.md#mc-02-the-journal-shippers-hlc-watermark-skips-entries-that-are-ingested-late-checkin-then-reports-0-pending)

#### MC-01 Outbox entries from event A are applied to event B when the operator switches competition mid-drain

*Area 02 (Multi-competition isolation) · functional / cross-competition corruption · **critical** · size S · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [API-02](areas/01-api-auth.md#api-02-eventspush-ignores-the-entrys-competitionid-and-keys-idempotency-by-bare-id-offline-entries-land-permanently-in-the-wrong-competition), [BLD-04](areas/11-build-tests.md#bld-04-an-outbox-drain-that-spans-a-competition-switch-writes-the-rest-of-the-queue-into-the-other-competition)
- **Related:** SYNC-04, MC-20, MC-17, SYNC-25
- **Location:** `packages/api/src/routers/events.ts:88-151`: `ingestJournalEntries`. The idempotency lookup is at `:99-106`, the apply at `:108`, the journal row at `:122-134`. `events.ts:69`: the schema carries `competitionId`, which is never read. `events.ts:229-240`: `push`. …
- **What is wrong:** The server applies every pushed entry to `ctx.event` from the header and never compares `entry.competitionId` with `event.nameId`. `drainEventQueue(competitionId)` loops over 50-entry batches filtered by the competition captured when the drain started. Each `trpcVanillaClient.events.push` reads the header from the URL at send time. The loop is not cancelled when the shell unmounts; `cancelled` only guards `setState`. Idempotency is `journalEntry.findUnique({ id })` on a global primary key. A misapplied entry therefore reports `synced` for ever, even when it is later pushed under the right header. The module-level `draining` flag also makes `drainEventQueue(B)` return 0 while A's drain runs, and the effect does not fire again.
- **Scenario:** On a two-race day the laptop has 120 pending `card.read` / `finish.recorded` / `runner.registered` entries for `race-am` after an outage. Wi-Fi returns and the drain starts. While batch 1 is in flight, the operator opens `race-pm` in the same tab. Batches 2 and 3 go out with `x-competition-id: race-pm`. `resolveRunner` matches by `(eventId, cardNo)` or by seq, so PM runners with the same cards get AM finish times. `runner.registered` creates AM runners in the PM class with the same seq. All entries are marked synced locally. `race-am` never gets them.
- **Fix (S):** Server: in `ingestJournalEntries`, before the idempotency lookup, push `{ id, error: "EVENT_MISMATCH" }` into `failed` and `continue` when `entry.competitionId !== event.nameId`. The node shipper already stamps `event.nameId` (`shipper.ts:83-106`, `events.ts:209`), so peers are unaffected. Also make the lookup `{ id, eventId }`-aware, and fail loudly when the id exists under another event. Client: add `trpcClientForEvent(nameId)`, a vanilla client cached per nameId whose `headers` and `scopeTrpcUrl` use the given nameId, never `window.location`. `drainEventQueue` groups pending entries by `competitionId` and pushes each group through its own client. Tests: an integration test in `events-push.test.ts` that pushes an entry whose `competitionId` differs from the header and expects `failed` with no rows written; a web unit test with two competitions pending and the location set to the other one.
- **Status:** confirmed from code by two independent verifiers, one on the server side and one on the client side.
- **Full entry:** [MC-01](areas/02-multi-competition.md#mc-01-outbox-entries-from-event-a-are-applied-to-event-b-when-the-operator-switches-competition-mid-drain)

#### EVT-01 Re-sync cancels runners who already have a result

*Area 07 (Eventor and import/export) · functional (data corruption mid-event) · **critical** · size S · confirmed* · *raised to critical in the merge (the area rated it high; see the note in the summary)*

- **Also reported as:** [API-04](areas/01-api-auth.md#api-04-eventorsync-cancels-every-runner-missing-from-eventors-entry-list-with-no-guard-for-an-odd-response-or-for-runners-who-already-have-a-result), [API-10](areas/01-api-auth.md#api-10-eventorsync-never-marks-results-only-runners-as-seen-so-the-second-sync-cancels-them), [MC-12](areas/02-multi-competition.md#mc-12-eventorsync-cancels-finished-runners-cancels-results-only-runners-on-every-sync-and-overwrites-local-corrections), [DB-08](areas/03-prisma-migrations.md#db-08-eventor-sync-can-mass-cancel-runners-and-reverts-desk-edits)
- **Related:** EVT-02, RES-02
- **Location:** `packages/api/src/routers/eventor.ts:1183-1199`: the withdrawn pass checks only `r.status !== cancel`. `:958-983`: the `existing` query has no `removed` filter. `:1140`: `seen` is filled only in the entries loop. The results-only loop (`:1144-1181`) never adds to it. …
- **What is wrong:** every runner with an `eventorPersonId` that is missing from this sync's entry list is set to Cancelled. The code never looks at the local status, finish time or card. A runner who appears in Eventor only in the result list is also cancelled, because the results loop never marks it as seen, and it stays cancelled on every later sync. The previous status is not kept, although the comment at `:927-930` claims audit history.
- **Scenario:** A club swaps the competitor on an entry in Eventor, or deletes the entry, after the import. The original runner still runs and is read out OK. The organiser presses Sync to pull late entries. That runner becomes Cancelled and drops out of the results. If Eventor returns a 200 with an empty `<EntryList/>`, every Eventor-imported runner is cancelled in one click.
- **Fix (S):** In the withdrawn pass, skip runners that have a finish time, a `cardId` or a status other than unknown/no-timing, and skip `removed` rows. Call `seen.add(result.personId)` in the results loop. Refuse the pass when `entries.length === 0 && existing.length > 0`. Add integration tests beside `eventor-reentry.test.ts`: a finished runner missing from the snapshot keeps its status, and an empty snapshot cancels nobody.
- **Status:** Confirmed The verifier traced the whole `sync` mutation and found no guard. Not run.
- **Full entry:** [EVT-01](areas/07-eventor.md#evt-01-re-sync-cancels-runners-who-already-have-a-result)

#### DB-01 Per-event `seq` counter is not raised by explicit-seq inserts, so creates fail permanently after a lease

*Area 03 (Prisma schema and migrations) · stability · **critical** · size S–M · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [SYNC-02](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-02-event_seqs-counters-never-travel-back-to-the-cloud-so-cloud-inserts-collide-after-checkin-and-a-re-checkout-carries-the-stale-counters-back-to-the-box)
- **Location:** `packages/api/prisma/migrations/20260517_init/migration.sql:141-155`: `allocate_event_seq`, the only definition. `packages/api/src/routers/events.ts:505-508`: `runner.registered` apply. `packages/api/src/routers/events.ts:860-864`: class, course and control upserts. `packages/api/src/routers/lease.ts:224-252`: checkin. …
- **What is wrong:** The trigger only touches `event_seqs` when `NEW.seq` is 0 or NULL. Journal apply inserts runners, classes, courses and controls with the origin's explicit `seq`, so the receiving node's counter never moves. The path is `runner.ts:477-484` journals `seq`, `sync/shipper.ts:106` pushes it, and `events.ts:507` inserts it. The doc says "At checkin the counters travel back with the barrier". Checkin sends no counters: it only ships, checks pending, and releases. `runner.create` (`runner.ts:425`) has no P2002 retry. The failed insert rolls back the counter increment, so the counter never advances.
- **Scenario:** The cloud holds runners 1–500, with `next_seq` = 501. The venue box checks out, registers runners 501–530, ships and checks in. The cloud still has `next_seq` = 501. The next `runner.create` on the cloud gets 501 and fails on `UNIQUE (event_id, seq)`. Every later create fails the same way until someone edits `event_seqs` by hand. A re-checkout (day 2 of a multi-day event) exports the stale counter. The box's first registration at the arena then fails the same way. Classes, courses and controls created on the box behave the same, as does every row after `forceTakeover`.
- **Fix (S–M):** Add a migration that gives `allocate_event_seq()` an `ELSE` branch for an explicit seq: `INSERT … VALUES (NEW.event_id, TG_TABLE_NAME, NEW.seq + 1) ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE SET next_seq = GREATEST(event_seqs.next_seq, EXCLUDED.next_seq)`. This covers every table, `forceTakeover` and late drains. In the same migration, repair every `(event, table)` to `MAX(seq) + 1`. Make `importEventSnapshot`'s `eventSeq.createMany` an upsert, because the trigger will already have created those rows. Add an integration test: the venue registers a runner and checks in, then `runner.create` succeeds on the cloud; then re-checkout and register on the box. Correct the doc sentence.
- **Status:** confirmed (reproduced). A private PG16 cluster with the exact function: after explicit seqs 3 and 4, `next_seq` stayed at 3, and two successive default inserts both failed with `duplicate key … (event_id, seq)=(1, 3)`. …
- **Full entry:** [DB-01](areas/03-prisma-migrations.md#db-01-per-event-seq-counter-is-not-raised-by-explicit-seq-inserts-so-creates-fail-permanently-after-a-lease)

#### RES-03 A redraw rewrites the start times of runners who have already started or finished

*Area 04 (ZeroTime, results and draw) · functional (data corruption) · **critical** · size M · confirmed*

- **Location:** `packages/api/src/draw/drawEngine.ts:75-81`: the runner filter is only `removed: false` and not withdrawn. `packages/api/src/routers/drawRouter.ts:165-170`: unconditional write of `startTime` and `startNo`. `packages/web/src/components/DrawPanel.tsx:104`: every non-free-start class is preselected. …
- **What is wrong:** `draw.execute` overwrites start time and start number for every selected runner, including those with a finish time, a status, punches or a hand-set start. Results compute finish minus start live (`packages/shared/src/results.ts:107-110`, `lists.ts:159,172`). A later re-readout does not repair it, because the stored start wins over the card's start punch (`readout.ts:325`) and is persisted again (`cardReadout.ts:907-913`).
- **Scenario:** mid-event, the organiser opens Draw to give a late class times. All classes are preselected; they press Preview, then Apply. Every finished runner's running time changes, or goes negative and drops out of the results. The old values survive only in earlier `start.adjusted` journal rows.
- **Fix (M):** Server: skip runners with `finishTime != 0`, a status other than Unknown, or a read card, unless `force: true`, and return the count skipped. Add an "only runners without a start time" mode for late entries. UI: preselect only undrawn classes, and when a selected class already has start times, confirm with the number that will be replaced. Integration test: a finished runner's start time survives a redraw.
- **Status:** confirmed (proven from code). It takes two deliberate clicks (Preview, then Apply).
- **Full entry:** [RES-03](areas/04-zerotime-results.md#res-03-a-redraw-rewrites-the-start-times-of-runners-who-have-already-started-or-finished)

#### RES-02 Eventor import writes absolute times into ZeroTime-relative columns, and a re-sync overwrites every runner

*Area 04 (ZeroTime, results and draw) · functional (data corruption) · **critical** · size M · confirmed*

- **Related:** EVT-01, EVT-02
- **Location:** `packages/api/src/eventor.ts:332-340` and `:963-964`: `timeToMeosDs` returns time since midnight. Stored without `toRelative` at `packages/api/src/routers/eventor.ts:802-803`, `:844-845`, `:1082-1083`, `:1127-1128` and `:1172-1173`. Re-sync at `routers/eventor.ts:942-945` and `:1041-1085`. Readers add ZeroTime, for example `runner.ts:260-261`.
- **What is wrong:** start and finish times taken from Eventor's `results/event` are stored as clock time in columns that every reader treats as relative to ZeroTime. In the re-sync, `needsUpdate` compares the relative stored value with the absolute Eventor value, so it is always true and every existing runner is rewritten from Eventor.
- **Scenario:** results are uploaded to Eventor mid-event, then the organiser runs an Eventor re-sync to pull late entries. Every runner's start, finish and status is replaced by Eventor's copy, so local corrections made after the upload are lost. All times shift by +9 h. A 10:00 start reads back as 19:00. For starts after about 15:00 the shifted value wraps past midnight, so finish < start and the running time is 0. Readout matching gets a wrong fallback start, so splits are wrong. Importing an already-run event shows the same +ZeroTime shift. …
- **Fix (M):** Apply `toRelative(…, event.zeroTime)` on all five writes. Compare like with like in `needsUpdate`. Separately decide whether a re-sync may overwrite local results at all. Suggested: never overwrite a runner that has a local readout or a non-Unknown status, and import only entries and cards. Add an integration test: import with results, then check that the start list shows the Eventor clock times; re-sync after a local correction, then check that the correction survives.
- **Status:** confirmed (proven from code by the verifier who found it as a side lead). This area's reviewer did not cover it; the Eventor-area review should cross-check it.
- **Full entry:** [RES-02](areas/04-zerotime-results.md#res-02-eventor-import-writes-absolute-times-into-zerotime-relative-columns-and-a-re-sync-overwrites-every-runner)

#### API-01 `events.push` applies every journaled write with no capability check (proxy mode)

*Area 01 (API routers and auth) · security · **critical** · size M · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [MC-03](areas/02-multi-competition.md#mc-03-eventspush-has-no-capability-check-any-signed-in-account-can-rewrite-or-wipe-any-event), [SI-07](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-07-eventspush-applies-result-runner-and-course-entries-for-any-signed-in-user-with-no-capability-check), [SYNC-04](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-04-eventspush-has-no-capability-check-ignores-each-entrys-competition-and-writes-unvalidated-fields-including-cross-event-upserts-by-uuid)
- **Related:** API-29, API-36
- **Location:** `packages/api/src/routers/events.ts:229` (procedure), apply switch `packages/api/src/routers/events.ts:345-798`; root `packages/api/src/trpc.ts:164` (`eventProcedure = authedProcedure.use(requireEvent)`); documented at `docs/system/authentication.md:231`.
- **What is wrong:** `push` calls `ingestJournalEntries(ctx.db, ctx.event, input.events)` and nothing else; neither it nor `applyEvent` reads `ctx.user` or capabilities. The same writes through their own procedures are gated: `runner.create/update/delete` are `manageRaceProcedure` (`runner.ts:417/509/588`), `cardReadout.applyResult` is `raceOperateRaceProcedure` (`cardReadout.ts:805`), `course.importCourses` is `coursesEditRaceProcedure` (`course.ts:1780`). `AUTH_AUTO_PROVISION` (`auth.ts:66,128-141`, set on the Cloud Run deploy) creates a grant-less member for anyone the proxy admits.
- **Scenario:** proxy mode, user U is a plain member with no grant on event `sm-2026`. U sends `events.push` with `x-competition-id: sm-2026` and entries `runner.deleted {runnerId: 1..2000}` (resolved by seq, `events.ts:285-311`), or `result.applied {...status: 4}`, or `reference.imported {replaceAll: true, courses: [], controls: []}`. Every runner is soft-deleted, results are overwritten, or every course and control is removed and every class loses its course (`events.ts:694-722`). Each equivalent direct procedure would answer FORBIDDEN.
- **Fix (M):** in `push`, resolve `capabilitiesForContext(ctx)` once and require a capability per entry type before applying: `card.read`, `finish.*`, `start.*`, `result.applied`, `punch.*` need `race.operate`; `runner.registered|updated|deleted` need `race.operate` or `event.manage` (owner decision: registration desks queue `runner.*` through the outbox, and mapping them to `event.manage` only would strand race-crew desks); `class.upserted` needs `event.manage`; `course.upserted`, `control.upserted`, `reference.imported` need `courses.edit`. Unknown types default to deny; an empty `events` array stays allowed (the client reads `serverTimeMs`). Refused entries go to `failed` with a FORBIDDEN code. …
- **Status:** Confirmed (from code: `events.ts:229`, `trpc.ts:164`, apply paths `events.ts:566-575`, `:390-415`, `:694-722`; `events-push.test.ts` has no auth assertion).
- **Full entry:** [API-01](areas/01-api-auth.md#api-01-eventspush-applies-every-journaled-write-with-no-capability-check-proxy-mode)


### High (62)

#### MC-04 The react-query cache, in memory and persisted, is not partitioned by event; offline you see and read out against another event's data

*Area 02 (Multi-competition isolation) · functional / competition mix-up · **high** · size M · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [WEB-01](areas/10-web-client.md#web-01-the-query-cache-is-neither-keyed-nor-cleared-by-competition-after-a-switch-competition-b-shows-as-cached-rows-with-as-per-event-ids-and-edits-on-them-hit-other-runners-in-b), [SYNC-19](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-19-the-stations-query-cache-is-not-scoped-by-competition-so-offline-readout-uses-another-competitions-runners-and-courses)
- **Related:** WEB-02, WEB-32, WEB-45, SYNC-03, MC-19
- **Location:** `packages/web/src/lib/offline/persister.ts:4`: one IndexedDB key, `oxygen-query-cache`. `packages/web/src/main.tsx:76-87`: no `buster`, maxAge 24 h. …
- **What is wrong:** Event-scoped queries have input `undefined`, so their keys carry no event. The only guard is `utils.invalidate()` plus `reset()` on shell mount, and both are skipped offline. Online, `invalidate` only marks queries stale. Inactive queries keep A's data under B until a refetch, and keep it if the refetch fails on a flaky network where `navigator.onLine` is still true. The persister writes the whole cache under one key, so two tabs on different events overwrite each other. Nothing calls `queryClient.clear()` or `removeQueries`.
- **Scenario:** On day 1 the laptop runs `ds-day1`. Day 2 is a separate event, `ds-day2`. In the morning, with no network yet, the operator opens `/ds-day2`. The persisted cache (under 24 h old) makes the shell "ready" with day 1's name, runners, classes and courses. Offline readouts are judged against day 1's courses and queued with `competitionId: ds-day2` and day 1's runner seqs.
- **Fix (M):** Persist per event (`createIdbPersister(nameId)` with key `oxygen-query-cache:<nameId>`), plus one small cache for non-event pages. When the event changes, call `queryClient.clear()` and restore that event's blob, instead of invalidate/reset. Add `nameId` to the dashboard payload and accept a cached dashboard offline only when it matches. Add an e2e test: visit A, go offline, open B, and expect "not available offline" rather than A's data.
- **Status:** confirmed from code.
- **Full entry:** [MC-04](areas/02-multi-competition.md#mc-04-the-react-query-cache-in-memory-and-persisted-is-not-partitioned-by-event-offline-you-see-and-read-out-against-another-events-data)

#### SI-04 SI8, SI9 and pCard punches are read from the wrong card offset

*Area 05 (SI readout and BLE reader) · functional · **high** · size M · confirmed (part plausible)*

- **Location:** `web/lib/si-protocol.ts:821-878` (`parseSI8CardData`, `startOffset = bi === 0 ? 32 : 0` at :850; the loop runs until `punches.length === punchCount` at :848, :855) …
- **What is wrong:** the parser assumes punches start at block 0 byte 32. On real cards that area is owner data, and punches start at byte 136 for SI8, byte 56 for SI9 and byte 176 for pCard. The parser only works when the owner area happens to be blank, because blank records are skipped.
- **Scenario:** SI8 2220164 carries owner data "2220164;SPORTident Swede". Ground truth is 48, 56, 53, 41, 57, 44, 45, 31, 32, 100. With 2 blocks the parser returns `50@03:34:08 54@15:42:51 336@17:38:26 361@07:08:21 372@02:17:55 357@19:08:21 48 56 53 41`: six garbage punches decoded from ASCII, and the last six real punches lost. A live read fetches block 0 only (10 ≤ 24) and returns only the six garbage punches. …
- **Fix (M):** add a per-series layout table keyed on the series byte `b0[24]`: SI9 (1) first punch 56, max 50; SI8 (2) 136, max 30; pCard (4) 176, max 20; tCard handled separately. Always read both blocks for these types (`BLOCKS_TO_READ` already says 2), concatenate them, and iterate by slot index `0..punchCount-1` (this also fixes SI-24). Replace the synthetic fixtures with assertions of the full punch lists for both SI8 cards in the capture against `readouts.txt`, fix the e2e mock, and correct `readout-backup-format.md:80-82, 125-129`.
- **Status:** confirmed and reproduced for SI8. The SI9 (56) and pCard (176) offsets are plausible: they follow the published SPORTident layout, but the repo has no capture of those cards.
- **Full entry:** [SI-04](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-04-si8-si9-and-pcard-punches-are-read-from-the-wrong-card-offset)

#### SI-05 A timed-out or interrupted USB readout is stored as complete, with the OK beep

*Area 05 (SI readout and BLE reader) · stability · **high** · size S–M · confirmed*

- **Location:** `web/lib/webserial.ts:549-557` (the block timeout calls `finalizeReadout`), `:631-677` (`finalizeReadout` never compares `pendingBlocks.length` with `pendingTotalBlocks` …
- **What is wrong:** when the next block does not arrive within 2 s, whatever arrived is parsed, ACKed (the station beeps OK) and dispatched as a normal `si:card-readout`. The parsers return fewer punches with no flag, and the server stores `punchCount: input.punches.length` (`api/routers/cardReadout.ts:678`), so the shortfall cannot be detected later.
- **Scenario:** an SIAC with 40 punches needs blocks 0–5. The runner pulls the card after block 4. Oxygen emits 32 of 40 punches with the finish time (from block 0), the match score is well above 0.2, and MP is written with an OK beep. If the card is pulled during blocks 1–3, the readout has zero punches and a finish time.
- **Fix (S–M):** in `finalizeReadout`, require `pendingBlocks.length === pendingTotalBlocks` before ACK and dispatch. Otherwise reset the state, `console.warn` the card number and the blocks received, and emit `si:card-read-failed`, which the kiosk and operator screen show as "insert the card again". CARD_REMOVED should abort the pending read at once. Optionally re-request a missing block once. As a second line of defence, DeviceManager should reject a readout whose `punches.length < punchCount` (this also covers Bluetooth). Add a unit test with a fake port (see SI-31).
- **Status:** confirmed from code.
- **Full entry:** [SI-05](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-05-a-timed-out-or-interrupted-usb-readout-is-stored-as-complete-with-the-ok-beep)

#### SI-10 A manual finish from the Finish Station and the card readout conflict, and a later read erases the manual finish

*Area 05 (SI readout and BLE reader) · functional · **high** · size M · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [RES-12](areas/04-zerotime-results.md#res-12-finish-station-the-online-and-offline-paths-give-different-results)
- **Related:** RES-01, WEB-53, WEB-72
- **Location:** `api/routers/race.ts:320-386` (`recordFinish` writes only `finishTime` at :346); `packages/shared/src/readout.ts:326` (`finishTime = finishPunch?.time ?? 0`); `api/routers/cardReadout.ts:238-266, 832-839`; `api/routers/events.ts:352-366` …
- **What is wrong:** `recordFinish` writes the finish time and returns `performReadout`, which ignores `runner.finishTime` and takes the finish only from a finish punch. A runner without a finish punch therefore comes back as DNF with running time 0, and the row is left with a finish time and status `unknown`. Offline, `finish.recorded` sets `status: "ok"` and first write wins, while the online path journals `finish.adjusted` with last write wins. The two paths disagree. …
- **Scenario:** the finish unit fails, so the crew times runners on the Finish Station. The screen says "finished" with time 0. At readout the card has all controls but no finish punch, so the kiosk shows DNF and the manual finish is set to 0.
- **Fix (M):** in `performReadout`, when there is no finish punch and `runner.finishTime != 0`, use the runner's time (converted with `toAbsolute`) and flag `finishSource: "manual"`. Give the online and offline manual finish one status rule and one overwrite rule. Never write finish 0 over a non-zero finish, in `applyResult` or in the `result.applied` ingest (SI-01). On the client, capture the time once at the key press, queue on any network-class error (not on NOT_FOUND or auth errors), and guard Enter with `isPending`.
- **Status:** confirmed from code.
- **Full entry:** [SI-10](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-10-a-manual-finish-from-the-finish-station-and-the-card-readout-conflict-and-a-later-read-erases-the-manual-finish)

#### SI-11 Pushing a readout-station backup stores the card but never applies a result

*Area 05 (SI readout and BLE reader) · functional · **high** · size S–M · confirmed*

- **Location:** `api/routers/cardReadout.ts:1572-1655` (`pushReadoutBackup`: only `storeReadoutImpl` at :1607, the flag at :1620 and the journal at :1627); `web/pages/BackupPunchesPage.tsx:98-100`; `docs/system/registration-and-readout.md:897, 912`
- **What is wrong:** nothing writes the runner's status, start or finish, and the page only refetches. The doc (§11 step 5) promises "result computation … as if the readout had just happened live". The result lists read the stored row (`api/routers/lists.ts:171-178`), so pushed runners have no result. The on-demand `readout` query does show a computed result, which makes the gap easy to miss.
- **Scenario:** Oxygen was down and runners read out on a standalone station. Afterwards the operator imports and pushes 300 backups. All show "pushed", the results list is still empty, and the operator has to edit 300 runners by hand.
- **Fix (S–M):** in the same transaction, when `linkedRunnerId` is set, run `performReadout` and write the result plus a `result.applied` journal entry as `applyResult` does. `acceptVariant` at ~:890 is the in-transaction pattern to copy; use the shared helper from the summary. Skip when the read is not relevant, apply the SI-01 guards, and add a "push all pending" button. Add status and time assertions to `api/__tests__/integration/readout-backup.test.ts:119-152` and correct the doc.
- **Status:** confirmed from code.
- **Full entry:** [SI-11](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-11-pushing-a-readout-station-backup-stores-the-card-but-never-applies-a-result)

#### RES-07 Journal replay of `result.applied` turns every status except OK, MP and DNF into Unknown

*Area 04 (ZeroTime, results and draw) · functional · **high** · size S · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [API-09](areas/01-api-auth.md#api-09-resultapplied-replay-maps-only-okmpdnf-every-other-status-becomes-unknown), [DB-03](areas/03-prisma-migrations.md#db-03-resultapplied-replay-collapses-most-statuses-to-unknown), [SI-08](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-08-resultapplied-ingest-maps-every-status-except-ok-mp-and-dnf-to-unknown), [SYNC-06](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-06-journal-replay-of-resultapplied-maps-every-status-except-ok-mp-and-dnf-to-unknown)
- **Location:** `packages/api/src/routers/events.ts:391-413`; the ternary at `:404-411` maps only 1, 3 and 4. `valueToRunnerStatus` is imported at `:43` but used only for `finish.adjusted` (`:383`).
- **What is wrong:** `applyResult` (`cardReadout.ts:844`), variant accept (`:927`) and the offline fallback (`DeviceManager.tsx:521`) journal the full numeric status. The replay path serves both `events.push` and the shipper pull (`ingestJournalEntries`, `:88` and `:108`), and it collapses the other statuses.
- **Scenario:** at the venue a runner is DQ'd (5), is over the maximum time (6), is out of competition (15), or runs in a no-timing class (2). The venue row is correct, but the cloud copy says Unknown with a finish time. Anything served from the cloud (public results, an Eventor upload from the cloud) then shows that runner as OK (see RES-21) or unplaced.
- **Fix (S):** use `valueToRunnerStatus(status)`. Add cases for statuses 2, 5, 6 and 15 to `__tests__/integration/events-push.test.ts`.
- **Status:** confirmed (proven from code).
- **Full entry:** [RES-07](areas/04-zerotime-results.md#res-07-journal-replay-of-resultapplied-turns-every-status-except-ok-mp-and-dnf-into-unknown)

#### API-03 Journal reference upserts address rows by bare UUID and pass client `fields` to Prisma: cross-event write, mass assignment, non-atomic child replace

*Area 01 (API routers and auth) · security · **high** · size M · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [MC-07](areas/02-multi-competition.md#mc-07-applyreferenceupsert-writes-by-row-uuid-with-no-event-check-and-passes-wire-fields-straight-into-prisma-update), [DB-04](areas/03-prisma-migrations.md#db-04-eventspush-reference-upserts-can-overwrite-another-events-courses-classes-and-controls)
- **Related:** SYNC-04
- **Location:** `packages/api/src/routers/events.ts:843-915` (`applyReferenceUpsert`): control `:866-870`, course `:875-881`, class `:898-904`; payload schema `packages/api/src/routers/events.ts:76` …
- **What is wrong:** `db.control.upsert({ where: { id: payload.id }, create, update: fields })` (same for course and class) never checks that an existing row belongs to `eventId`. `fields` is the raw `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())` payload, so any column is writable, including `eventId`, `seq`, `id`, `removed`, `parentCourseId`, `finishControlId`, class `courseId`. …
- **Scenario:** a caller bound to event A pushes `course.upserted {id: <B's course>, seq: 0, fields: {removed: true}, courseControls: []}`: B's course is removed and its `course_controls` rows deleted. `fields: {eventId: <A's id>}` moves B's course into A. …
- **Fix (M):** at the top of `applyReferenceUpsert`, load the row by id; if it exists with `eventId !== eventId` throw "row belongs to another event"; if it does not exist, create under the local `eventId` (bigint ids differ per node, UUIDs are shared, so this is the right test). Strip `id`, `eventId`, `seq`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` from `fields` and whitelist against what `portableFields` in `referenceJournal.ts` emits (that set still includes `version`, `removed`, `parentCourseId`, `variantKey`, `finishControlId`, class `courseId`, `structure`). Check that FK-valued fields and every `courseControls[].controlId` / `coursePools[].courseId` belong to the event. …
- **Status:** Confirmed (reproduced: throwaway integration test against `oxygen_test`, 4/4 assertions passed).
- **Full entry:** [API-03](areas/01-api-auth.md#api-03-journal-reference-upserts-address-rows-by-bare-uuid-and-pass-client-fields-to-prisma-cross-event-write-mass-assignment-non-atomic-child-replace)

#### API-07 `lease.checkout` deletes a local event copy that still has un-shipped journal entries

*Area 01 (API routers and auth) · stability (data loss) · **high** · size M · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [MC-09](areas/02-multi-competition.md#mc-09-leasecheckout-deletes-the-local-event-without-checking-for-unshipped-entries-and-requires-no-capability), [SYNC-07](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-07-checkout-deletes-the-local-event-without-checking-for-unshipped-data-and-needs-only-a-signed-in-user), [DB-18](areas/03-prisma-migrations.md#db-18-lease-checkout-wipes-unshipped-local-entries-and-a-local-lease-can-become-impossible-to-release)
- **Related:** API-25, SYNC-18
- **Location:** missing guard `packages/api/src/routers/lease.ts:150-154` (before `peer.acquire`); delete `packages/api/src/sync/lease.ts:319` (`tx.event.deleteMany({ where: { nameId } })`, cascading `JournalEntry`, `EventLease`, `JournalSyncState`) …
- **What is wrong:** the doc says checkout "requires the venue to be fully shipped-up-to-date"; `checkout` never calls `shippingStatus` or `shipEventOnce` (compare `checkin`, `lease.ts:224-246`). The only implicit guard is the cloud's own active lease, which makes `peer.acquire` throw CONFLICT.
- **Scenario:** (a) UI path: the box checks in, then takes registrations or readouts locally (no lease means writable, `sync/lease.ts:75-87`). The shipper pushes every 5 s, but the panel shows the checkout button and no pending count when no lease is held. …
- **Fix (M):** in `checkout`, before `peer.acquire`, if the event exists locally by `nameId`, run `shipEventOnce` and refuse with PRECONDITION_FAILED when `shippingStatus(...).pendingPush > 0`, as `checkin` does. `shippingStatus` treats a missing `journalSyncState` row as watermark 0, so a locally created event with the same `nameId` and any journal entry is refused; decide whether that needs an explicit override. Decide the stale-local-lease state: refuse checkout while a local active lease exists, and let `checkin` tolerate "no active lease on the peer" once everything is shipped, otherwise the box stays stuck. Show the pending count in `VenueLeasePanel` when no lease is held. …
- **Status:** Confirmed (from code: `lease.ts:141-216` read in full, sole caller of `importEventSnapshot`, cascade in schema). Not run with two nodes.
- **Full entry:** [API-07](areas/01-api-auth.md#api-07-leasecheckout-deletes-a-local-event-copy-that-still-has-un-shipped-journal-entries)

#### SYNC-08 A box with no lease, or a stale one, is still a writer; its backlog overwrites newer cloud data, and it cannot end its lease after a takeover

*Area 06 (Offline sync and publishing) · stability (silent overwrite) · **high** · size M · confirmed*

- **Location:** `packages/api/src/sync/lease.ts:67-70,76-87`: no lease, or this node holds it, means writable; `NODE_ROLE` is not consulted. `packages/api/src/routers/lease.ts:260-281`: `forceTakeover` updates only the cloud's own row …
- **What is wrong:** After a force takeover the box keeps its active local lease. When its uplink returns, the shipper pushes hours-old `finish.adjusted`, `result.applied` and full-row upserts, which the cloud applies last-arrival-wins over corrections made after the takeover. Nothing is logged as a conflict; no conflict detection exists. The box cannot clear its stale lease through any API route. After a normal checkin, stations still pointed at the box keep writing …
- **Scenario:** the box's uplink drops at 10:00 while stations keep writing to it. At 12:00 the cloud force-takes over and the organiser corrects three results there. At 12:30 the link returns and the box ships its backlog; the three corrections are overwritten by older venue values, silently.
- **Fix (M):** On a `NODE_ROLE=venue` node, treat "event exists, no active local lease held by me" as not race-writable, with a typed error pointing at the cloud. In the shipper cycle, ask the peer for `lease.status`; if the peer's active holder is not this node, mark the local lease released (forced), stop accepting race writes, and keep shipping the backlog flagged as stale. On the cloud, when a register entry arrives from a node that does not hold the lease, quarantine it or apply only if its HLC is newer than the row's last write, and log one WARN line per entry. Make checkin put the local lease into a "checking-in" state that `assertRaceWritable` rejects, then ship, then release the peer. …
- **Status:** confirmed from code reading. Sub-claim 1 is high; 2 is medium (needs stations still on a box the runbook says to shut down); 3 is low. "LAN stations keep writing" needs the box alive but cut off …
- **Full entry:** [SYNC-08](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-08-a-box-with-no-lease-or-a-stale-one-is-still-a-writer-its-backlog-overwrites-newer-cloud-data-and-it-cannot-end-its-lease-after-a-takeover)

#### MC-05 The card reader's duplicate detection spans competitions; a card re-read in the correct event is never stored

*Area 02 (Multi-competition isolation) · functional / lost readout · **high** · size S · confirmed*

- **Also reported as:** [SI-16](areas/05-si-readout-ble.md#si-16-readout-dedup-has-no-time-window-and-survives-a-competition-switch-so-a-re-read-is-silently-not-stored)
- **Location:** `packages/web/src/context/DeviceManager.tsx`: `:268-290`: `setCompetitionNameId` does not touch `recentCardsRef`. `:313-321`: dedupe by `readoutsMatch`, with no time window and no event. `:357`: `!isDuplicate` gates `storeReadout`. …
- **What is wrong:** The recent-cards list lives for the whole page session. A second read of a card with identical punches is treated as a duplicate and not stored, although the first read was stored in a different event or nowhere. On the selector or settings page, a read stores nothing and queues nothing. The flow then falls through to local readout (`:531`) and can show a result computed from whatever event is cached (MC-04). `docs/system/registration-and-readout.md:131` says duplicates are detected "within a short window", but the code has no window.
- **Scenario:** The operator still has yesterday's event open, or is on the selector, and reads the first finisher's card. They notice, switch to today's event without reloading, and read the card again. The read is treated as a duplicate and no `storeReadout` reaches today's event, so the runner has no card data and no result. Re-reading does not help until the page is reloaded or the manual "clear" button is pressed.
- **Fix (S):** Clear `recentCards`, `recentCardsRef` and `currentCard` when the nameId changes. Record on each `RecentCard` the nameId it was stored under and whether the store or queue succeeded. Count a read as a duplicate only when both match. Show a visible "no competition open, card not saved" notice. Correct the doc sentence.
- **Status:** confirmed from code.
- **Full entry:** [MC-05](areas/02-multi-competition.md#mc-05-the-card-readers-duplicate-detection-spans-competitions-a-card-re-read-in-the-correct-event-is-never-stored)

#### SYNC-16 The service worker answers tRPC queries from a stale cache, so a finished runner's readout can be treated as pre-start

*Area 06 (Offline sync and publishing) · functional · **high** · size M · confirmed*

- **Location:** `packages/web/vite.config.ts:59-79` (same-origin `/trpc/*` GET, `NetworkFirst`, `networkTimeoutSeconds: 3`, 24 h); `packages/web/src/context/DeviceManager.tsx:429-431` (invalidate), `:435-441` (`readout.fetch`), `:448-473` (action decided …
- **What is wrong:** on a link slower than 3 s, or with the node unreachable, the service worker returns the last cached response for that exact URL as an ordinary 200. React Query stores it as fresh data, and nothing marks it as cached. The local fallback is never reached because the fetch "succeeded".
- **Scenario:** a runner checks her card at the readout station before the start (cached answer: found, no result, match score 0). After the race the uplink takes 5 s. `storeReadout` (a POST, not cached) succeeds; the readout GET times out in the service worker and gets the pre-start answer, so the action is `pre-start`, `applyResult` is not called and nothing is queued. …
- **Fix (M):** stop runtime-caching tRPC for race-critical procedures (the persisted query cache already serves offline reads), or restrict the route to an allow-list of slow-changing queries. At minimum, stamp cached responses (`x-oxygen-sw-cache: 1`) with a Workbox plugin and have the fetch wrapper throw a network-class error for stamped responses of must-be-live procedures (`cardReadout.readout`, `race.lookupByCard`, `runner.findByCard`, `event.counterState`). Add an E2E with a delayed route.
- **Status:** confirmed by code reading of the config and consumers; Workbox behaviour taken from its documented NetworkFirst semantics, not run in a browser. …
- **Full entry:** [SYNC-16](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-16-the-service-worker-answers-trpc-queries-from-a-stale-cache-so-a-finished-runners-readout-can-be-treated-as-pre-start)

#### SYNC-11 Queued writes are not retried after a failed drain

*Area 06 (Offline sync and publishing) · stability · **high** · size S–M · confirmed*

- **Location:** `packages/web/src/hooks/useEventQueue.ts:48-76` (effect deps `[isOnline, pendingCount > 0, competitionId, utils]`; the 2 s poll at `:31-45` never drains), `packages/web/src/lib/offline/sync.ts:63-67` (`catch` then `break`) …
- **What is wrong:** auto-drain runs only when `navigator.onLine` flips or the pending count goes from 0 to more than 0. The normal arena failure is "Wi-Fi associated, node unreachable", where `navigator.onLine` stays true. The first queued entry triggers one drain; if it fails, the loop breaks, entries stay `pending` (attempts not incremented) and nothing re-triggers. Whole-request failures (expired IAP session returning HTML, 413, 5xx, a zod rejection of the batch) take the same path and never become `failed`.
- **Scenario:** the venue box reboots (40 s). A readout station queues 6 card reads; if the drain request fails before the box is back, the 6 reads sit behind an amber "6" until someone presses sync, reloads, or leaves and re-enters the competition. New cards meanwhile go online normally.
- **Fix (S–M):** while `pendingCount > 0`, retry with capped exponential backoff (2 s to 30 s), plus on `visibilitychange`, on node-discovery change and after any successful tRPC call. Classify errors in `sync.ts` (network versus server answer) and, after N consecutive non-network failures, show a red state with the message. Add a fetch timeout on `trpcVanillaClient`. Unit-test with a fake client; Playwright test that aborts `events.push` once while online.
- **Status:** confirmed from code reading. Not deterministic for a 40 s reboot: with no fetch timeout the single request may hang and succeed when the box returns. The quoted "no silently stuck entries" is not in the doc …
- **Full entry:** [SYNC-11](areas/06-offline-sync.md#sync-11-queued-writes-are-not-retried-after-a-failed-drain)

Nothing retries when the link returns except a visibility change or a button press. …
